利得」、マイナスの場合は「交易損失」。公表値は 2020暦年連鎖価格のデフレーターから算出されているた 
め（基準年の交易利得・損失額はゼロ）、交易利得・損失額はあくまでも基準年からの交易条件の変化の影響

### Page 3

1

3 / 7

GDPデフレーターは前年同期比＋2.4％と 19四半期連続のプラスだった。また、単位労働コ
スト（＝名目雇用者報酬÷実質 GDP）は同＋4.4％と 14四半期連続のプラスで、賃金面からの
物価上昇圧力は足元でも継続している（巻末の関連指標①下段左）。

個人消費は、4-6月期が下方修正された上で 2四半期連続の減少 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と 2四半期連続で減少した。公表前の 4-6月期の個人消費は同＋
0.0％だったが、インバウンド消費額が上方修正されたことで同▲0.3％へと引き下げられた2。
7-9月期は一部耐久財の反動減や、9 月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因があったもの
の、所得環境が改善する中で 4-6月期から 2四半期連続で減少したところに、個人消費の弱さ
が見られる。 
国内家計最終消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると、耐久財（前期比▲3.5％）や半耐久財
（同▲1.4％）、非耐久財（同▲0.2％）がいずれも減少した一方、サービス（同＋0.1％）は増
加した（巻末の関連指標①上段左）。内閣府によると、耐久財ではエアコンなどが、半耐久財
では衣服などが、非耐久財ではその他個人ケア用器具（歯ブラシなど）などが押し下げたとい
う。4-6月期には、自動車税の環境性能割廃止や、2027年 4月に予定されているエアコンの省
エネ基準引き上げを見越した買い替え需要が耐久財消費を後押ししたが、その効果がはく落し
た。衣料品などの不振は天候不順が影響したとみられる。一方、サービスでは教育や携帯電話
通信料などが押し上げたという。 
実質雇用者報酬（家計最終消費支出デフレーターで実質化）は前期比＋0.3％と6四半期連続
で増加した（巻末の関連指標①上段右）。労働力調査（総務省）に見る雇用者数は同 0.0％で、
雇用者数で除した1人あたり実質雇用者報酬は同＋0.3％と6四半期連続で増加した（前年同期
比では3四半期連続のプラス）。

設備投資は3四半期ぶりの増加、住宅投資は2四半期連続の減少 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と 3四半期ぶりに増加した。建設投資は減少したものの、4-6月期
に特殊要因で減少していた研究開発投資で反動増が表れたほか、機械投資が増加したようだ。
内閣府によると、機械投資はサーバーや半導体製造装置などが増加に寄与したという。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。原材料費高騰による建設資材価格の上
昇や人件費の上昇が重しとなっており、住宅着工戸数は 6月にかけて増加していたものの、7
月以降は下落に転じている（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。

2 家計最終消費支出は国内消費支出から非居住者家計の国内での直接購入（インバウンド消費）を控除して算
出されているため、インバウンド消費の上方修正は家計最終消費支出の下方修正につながる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

公共投資は 2四半期連続の減少、政府消費は6四半期ぶりに減少 
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。資材価格の高騰や人手不足などが重し
になっている上、2025年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一巡したとみられる。 
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と小幅ながらも6四半期ぶりに減少した。内閣府によると、医療・
介護費が増加したものの、中間消費を中心にデフレーターが上昇したことが実質成長率の押し
下げにつながったという。 
また、実質 GDP成長率に対する公的在庫変動の寄与度は、7-9月期は大規模な石油の国家備
蓄放出が行われなかったため前期比＋0.5％ptとなり、成長率を大幅に押し上げた。後述のよ
うに原油の代替調達の進展によって、輸入の増加が実質 GDP成長率を押し下げたが、公的在庫
変動の増加がおおむね相殺した形だ。

輸出減と輸入増により、外需寄与度は前期比▲0.6％pt 
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と4四半期ぶりに減少した。財は増加したが、サービスが減少した。内
閣府によると、財では半導体製造装置や石油製品、基礎化学製品などが増加したという。サー
ビスでは、4-6月期に特殊要因で大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスに加え、インバウンド
や産業財産権等使用料なども減少したようだ。 
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と 2 四半期ぶりに増加した。サービスは減少したものの、財が増加し
た。財では、中東情勢の影響により 4-6月期に減少していた石油・天然ガスが代替調達の進展
によって増加したほか、非鉄金属やサーバー等も増加したようだ。サービスでは、アウトバウ
ンドは増加したものの、内閣府によると著作権等使用料や研究開発サービス、専門・経営コン
サルティングサービスなどが減少したという。 
輸出の減少と輸入の増加により、純輸出（外需）の実質 GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナスとなった。

10-12月期の実質 GDPは内需と輸出に支えられ、小幅のプラス成長か 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と、小幅ながらもプラス成長
に転じると見込んでいる。 
個人消費は小幅な増加に転じるとみている。現金給与総額は前年同月比で 3％を超える伸び
が続いており、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上げられていることから名目賃金の高い伸び
は続くだろう。一方、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっている。エネルギー価格について
も、資源価格高騰がラグを持って反映されることに加え、補助の一時終了や縮小も物価の押し
上げ要因となり、10-12月期の実質賃金上昇率を押し下げるだろう。設備投資は建設資材価格
と人件費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投資を背景に増加基調が

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

続くと見込む。 
輸出は増加に転じるとみている。米国の設備投資はAI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。
米国内での設備投資増加は米国向け輸出の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。
一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、増加は緩やかなものと
なるだろう。 
輸入は増加するとみている。原油輸入の回復が続くことに加え、AI関連需要を背景に電算機
類等の輸入も増加するだろう。輸入の増加が輸出の増加を上回ることで、外需の寄与度は 2四
半期連続のマイナスになると見込んでいる。 
10-12月期以降を含めたリスク要因は、AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化である。足元
の米国経済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などの IT
関連投資がけん引役となっている。AI関連サービスの世界的な利用拡大に伴い、計算能力の増
強に向けた大型投資が今後も続く可能性は高い。一方で、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務
面への懸念が広がっているほか、米中間選挙でAIデータセンター建設の一時停止や規制強化な
どを訴えた民主党急進左派候補者が当選するなど、AI投資を取り巻く環境には変化の兆しも見
られる。ハイパースケーラーが設備投資の拡大ペースを落とせば、半導体や情報通信機器など
の需要を下押しし、関連産業の生産減や輸出減などを通じて実体経済にも波及するだろう3。ま
た、中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格がさらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押
しするだろう。 
2027年度までの経済見通しの詳細などについては、11月 20日に発表予定の「第 231回日本
経済予測」を参照されたい。

3 詳細は、畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポー
ト、2026年11月 12日）を参照。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

AWSC’s 3Q26 core business OP was NT$209mn, which was lower 
than both GSe/BBG consensus at NT$226mn/NT$250mn, mainly 
due to the ongoing increase in raw material cost, leading to a 24.3% 
GM (lower than GSe/BBG consensus GM at 25.6%/27.8%), despite 
UTR in 3Q26 remaining stable at 65-70%. Moreover, AWSC reported 
NI of NT$191mn, which was 3% higher than GSe but 17% lower than 
the BBG consensus due to the GM contraction. 
Going into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue will increase by 
1% as key business lines (including WiFi, cellular and VCSEL) should 
remain stable in 4Q26 as we expect the company’s utilization rate to 
remain stable at 65-70%. Moreover, we expect its GM to rise to 
27.2% (up 2.9ppt QoQ) due to the material cost increase slowing 
down. 
We continue to view rising material costs as the key risk to AWSC’s 
profitability, as the company is now securing raw materials at higher 
cost as low-cost inventory has already been depleted in 1H26 amid 
the strong demand pull in. The company continues to negotiate 
price increases with clients and expects the adjustments to be fully 
reflected starting in 1Q27. However, we remain cautious on the 
low-end cellular segment, where customer demand tends to be 
more price-sensitive. 
For the AI-related business, the company continues to ship VCSEL 
products for 400G/800G optical transceiver this year, with revenue 
contribution to be <2% in 2026E per our estimates. Meanwhile, the 
company continues to make progress on the new PD (Photodiode) 
product and is currently undergoing verification with several 
customers. The company expects PD products to start seeing 
revenue contribution starting from 2H27 or 2027-end given its long 
verification process, typically 6 months+, thus we expect revenue 
contribution to be limited at <2%/~5% in 2027E/2028E. 
Investment view

### Page 3

We maintain our conservative view on the cellular/WiFi PA industry (see here) as we 
continue to see weaker smartphone shipment (-10%/+2%/+2% in 2026/27/28, see 
here) as the smartphone market continues to be impacted by the elevated memory price 
(see here). 
For AWSC, we believe the company could further gain market share among China PA 
design houses as Chinese clients increasingly shift toward higher-end PA products, 
supported by the company’s superior cost control capability and higher production yield 
relative to peers. In addition, AWSC has continued to expand its market share with a 
Korean smartphone brand, which should provide further growth momentum to its 
cellular business in coming quarters/years. Overall, we expect AWSC to continue gaining 
share from its key competitors due to (1) its competitive pricing strategy, underpinned 
by a more favorable cost structure comparing to its key competitor, Win Semi 
(AWSC/Win Semi’s fixed cost accounts for 30%/40% of the total, while Win Semi’s COGS 
per wafer is ~130% higher than AWSC’s) and (2) its strategic focus on China- and 
US-based PA design houses. 
We continue to see a recovery in 2026 CAPEX plans among Taiwan PA foundry players. 
However, we do not view this as a signal of a meaningfully stronger demand outlook as 
the capital spending is more related to new AI businesses. AWSC revised up its 2026 
CAPEX guidance to NT$600-650mn (from NT$500-600mn) and continues to expect 
CAPEX to increase in 2027/28. Also, management continues to expect UTR to reach at 
most ~70% in 2026, implying no need for capacity expansion in the near term while the 
additional capex will be spent on new optical related business. 
Overall, we maintain our Neutral rating on AWSC with a new 12-m TP of NT$136 (from 
NT$147) as we roll over our valuation period by one quarter. Post results, we revise 
down our 2027E/28E earnings by 10%/10% to reflect lower revenue and the negative 
impact from rising material cost (GM contraction), revise up 2026E earnings by 2%, and 
introduce our 2029E estimates.

Earnings revisions  
We revise up our 2026E revenue by 1% to factor in the 3Q26 actual results and we revise 
down 2027E/28E revenue by 9%/10% assuming a more conservative outlook for the 
cellular business; we revise down 2026E/27E/28E GM estimates by 0.4/0.7/0.8ppt to 
reflect the negative impact from rising material cost. Overall, we revise down 2027E/28E 
earnings by 10%/10% and revise up 2026E earnings by 2%. 
In this report, we introduce our 2029E estimates.

Valuation 
We maintain our Neutral rating and revise down our TP from NT$147 to NT$136, based 
on a lower 25x target P/E (from 27x previously, 0.5 STDV below the company’s past 
10-year forward P/E multiple), factoring in our earnings revisions and, consistent with 
our group coverage practices, rolling over our valuation period from 4Q26-3Q27E to 
2027E.

### Page 6

Investment Thesis and TP & risks

AWSC is one of the largest GaAS foundries globally, with 10-15% market share in the 
GaAS foundry industry in 2025. We expect the company to enjoy solid share gain 
opportunity in the long term, considering its much lower than peers’ production cost 
and better production efficiency. However, given the high inventory level in the supply 
chain, and the slower-than-expected Android smartphone PA demand in coming 
quarters, we expect relatively slow profit growth in the near term. 
We are Neutral rated given the company’s relatively slow profit growth in coming 
quarters. We think the stock is priced fairly, trading at its historical upcycle average P/E 
multiple, despite the long-term market share growth opportunity. We forecast AWSC’s 
net income to grow at a 34% 2026E-28E CAGR. The key up/downside risks are (1) 
easing/rising competition in the China PA foundry market, (2) 
faster/slower-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trends, and (3) 
faster/slower-than expected progress with new businesses, including LEO satellite, 
drones and datacom.  
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$136 is based on a target 25x 
2027E P/E, which is 0.5 STDV below the company’s past 10-year forward P/E multiple 
and the inventory build-up period average P/E. We consider this multiple appropriate 
given the soft demand outlook and our positive view on the company’s longer-term 
share gain opportunity. 
The key up/downside risks are 1) easing/rising competition in the China PA foundry 
market, (2) faster/slower-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trends, and (3) 
faster/slower-than expected progress with new businesses, including LEO satellite, 
drones and datacom.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Industry View In-Line
4Q26 iPhone builds unchanged: We maintain our 4Q26 iPhone build forecast at
72M units (+33% q/q, -5% y/y). While builds are expected to increase sequentially,
volumes remain below last year’s level, with softer legacy-model production,
including the iPhone 16/17e, weighing on the outlook. Our 2H26 build forecast for

the new iPhone 18 family remains unchanged at 71M units (-21% y/y), with the y/y
comparison also affected by one fewer SKU launched than last year. Within this, we
continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo builds. We will continue to monitor the
production ramp and sell-through data for potential upside or downside to our build
estimates. Our full-year forecast implies CY26 iPhone shipments of 234M units
(+3% y/y), with modest growth for the year despite the softer 4Q y/y outlook.

4Q26 iPad builds unchanged; year-end production outlook remains subdued: We
maintain our 4Q26 iPad build forecast at 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y), implying
continued pressure on production volumes into year-end. Our full-year estimate
remains 46.8M units (-14% y/y), with the sharper y/y contraction in 4Q pointing to a
weaker finish to the year.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 191: 261116_daiwa_MPI

Source PDF: all report pdf/261116_daiwa_MPI.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: MPI hosted its investor meeting on 16 November. 3Q26 
revenue was TWD5.9bn (+12.8% QoQ), 3.2% below our forecast but 2.1% 
above the Bloomberg consensus. Gross margin rose 3.3pp QoQ to 61.7%, 
beating our/consensus forecasts by 2.2pp, driven by a better product mix. 
Operating margin reached 38.6% (+4.1pp QoQ), 1.4/3.2pp above 
our/consensus forecasts. Management guided for 4Q26E/2027E revenue 
growth of 5-10% QoQ/50% YoY, with gross margin of c.62%/above 60%, 
respectively. We believe the company’s guidance is conservative and 
forecast 4Q26/2027 revenue to be TWD6.7bn/TWD37.7bn, with gross 
margin of 61.8%/61.5% and operating margin of 39.5%/39.9%, 
respectively.

What’s the impact: Probe card capacity expansion on track. MPI 
maintained its capacity expansion plan, targeting monthly VPC/MEMS 
capacities of 2.0m/3.5m probes by end-2026 and 2.5m/6.0m by end-2027. 
Management plans to release its final 2027 expansion plan by end-
December, with potential upside to current targets. Demand remains solid, 
with capacity still insufficient to meet customer needs and no signs of order 
cuts, per management. We believe testing intensity is the key driver of 
sector growth and we expect MPI's diversified customer base to limit the 
impact of order fluctuations from any single customer or project.

CPO contribution likely to accelerate in 2028. Although the market 
seems conservative about its CPO ramp-up, the company claimed that 
Insertion 2 qualification at a tier-1 customer remains on track, with 
qualification results expected by end-December and potential mass 
production orders in 1H27. For Insertion 3, qualification is progressing with 
multiple customers, with potential production orders in 1H27. Given 
qualification lead times, we forecast the CPO testing machine to contribute 
5-13% of revenue over 2027-28, aligned with management’s expectation 
that CPO will contribute a double-digit % in 2028.

What we recommend: We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 6-13% to reflect 
higher opex and cut our 4-quarter-forward target PER multiple from 65x to 
45x to reflect recent macro uncertainties, peer derating and moderating 
earnings growth. As a result, we lower our 12-month TP to TWD5,625 (from 
TWD8,415), while reaffirming our Buy (1) rating. Our target multiple implies 
a c.0.6 PEG based on our 2026-28E EPS CAGR of 73%. Given its 
diversified customer base and continued AI/HPC demand growth, we view 
current levels as offering an attractive risk-reward profile.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 4-5% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more conservative opex assumptions.

16 November 2026

Solid fundamentals; attractive entry post derating

➢ Capacity expansion on track; AI/HPC demand remains intact 
➢ CPO contribution likely to become more significant in 2028  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD5,625

Company profile 
Founded in Hsinchu in 1995, MPI is a leading global probe card supplier offering CPC, VPC and MEMS 
solutions, alongside photonics, semiconductor and thermal testing equipment. Growth is driven by 
expanding AI/HPC demand, rising MEMS adoption, capacity expansion, deeper vertical integration and 
emerging opportunities in CPO testing.

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다.

Document 281: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

4 November 2026

world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.
Business creations by sector: a boom that is building momentum and spreading

We have calculated the year-on-year change in the number of new business start-ups. The table categorises these results as positive (red), neutral (white) or negative (blue). The darker
the colour, the larger the change. The data series are defined as follows: Germany = new entries in the companies register (start-ups) since 2008; France = business start-ups, including

micro-entrepreneurs, since 2000; United Kingdom = businesses registered for VAT/PAYE since 2017; United States = business applications (tax identification number) since 2004. Sectors
have been grouped together to ensure that the classification is standardised across all countries. For the third quarter, the German data only include the months of July and August.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Document 348: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
All state Housing Sentiment Indexes are at weak levels.
However, November saw a bigger decline in Western
Australia (–4.8%mth) which now has the weakest housing-
related sentiment index across the major states at just 80.9.
Queensland and South Australia moved in line with the

national index. Moves were notably milder in New South
Wales (–1.7%) and especially Victoria (–0.7%) where the state
index, at 86.6, is the least pessimistic of the major states.
The sub-group detail showed a more pronounced weakening in
housing-related sentiment amongst high income earners and
consumers aged over 55 with sentiment holding about steady
amongst low-income earners, albeit at weaker overall levels.
Our November consumer sentiment survey also included

two new regular questions on consumers’ property buying
and selling intentions over the next twelve months. These
questions will continue to be run every month.
Despite the broader pessimism around housing, a relatively
high number of consumers are planning to buy in 2027.
Responses show 12.8% of consumers plan to buy a new
home over the next year. Comparable figures from Westpac’s
Home Ownership survey going back to 2019 suggest this is a
relatively firm result, up slightly since the middle of the year,

on par with the start of 2025 and above the levels reported
during the COVID pandemic in 2020-21. The result suggests
there is significant ‘latent demand’ for home purchases in
Australia, and that many buyers are just waiting for the right
opportunity. The state detail shows intentions are particularly
high in Queensland where one in five consumers is reportedly
planning to buy in 2027.
Interestingly, the positive intentions are not just around home

purchase with 11.2% of consumers reporting that they plan
to buy an investment property over the next year. Again,
comparable figures from Westpac’s Home Ownership survey
show this is up a tick compared to May and only marginally
below the 11.5% reporting intentions to buy investment
properties back in January 2025.
BULLETIN
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in
November from 85.3 in October.
The Index is a composite based on housing-related sentiment

measures from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer
Sentiment survey. It already appears regularly in Westpac’s
Housing Pulse reports but will now be published on a
monthly basis. The Index has proven to be an extremely useful
predictor of turning points in housing markets, both nationally
and across states. Westpac is also introducing two new
survey questions on consumers property buying and selling
intentions that will be run every month.
The November update shows housing-related sentiment

remains remain deeply pessimistic. At 85.3, the latest Index
read is in the bottom 3% of observations since 2005, when all
components first became available. That said, it is still above the
81.4 low in late 2022 and the 78.6 registered when the COVID
pandemic hit in 2020. Housing-related sentiment was markedly
weaker during the slump in the late 1980s/early 1990s.
Component-wise, the November sentiment decline centred on
a sharp downgrade to price expectations. As detailed below,

the Westpac Housing Sentiment Index composite is based
on four sub-indexes: one tracking assessments of ‘time to buy
a dwelling’; one tracking ‘consumer house price expectations’;
one tracking consumer expectations for unemployment and a
measure of risk aversion based on consumer assessments of
the ‘wisest place for savings’.
Consumer price expectations took a big step down in
November. The Westpac–Melbourne Institute Index of
House Price Expectations recorded a steep 13.4% drop

to 99.6, marking the second biggest monthly deterioration
since the COVID period and the first net negative (sub-100)
read since November 2022. The detailed responses show
consumers are now evenly split on the outlook, just over 38%
expecting more declines, 19% expecting no change and 38%
expecting a rise (5% reported “don’t know).
At least some consumers are starting to see lower prices as
a potential buying opportunity. The ‘time to buy a dwelling’

index rose 1.3% to 89.6 in November, to be up 1.3% on a year
ago as well but still at a very weak level overall, well below
the long run average of 114.
The wider economic backdrop was also less conducive for
housing-related sentiment. Consumers continue to report
a gradual rise in job-loss fears and risk aversion remains
elevated near historic highs. Both factors are likely to be
deterring prospective home buyers.
Housing sentiment sinks but buying intentions positive

“Despite the broader pessimism
around housing, a relatively high
number of consumers are planning
to buy ...”
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Conversely, just 8.2% of Australians are planning to sell a home 
over the next year. Comparable figures from Westpac’s Home 
Ownership survey suggest this is down significantly from 10.3% 
back in May and near the 7.7% low recorded at the start of 2024. 
On investment properties, 6.7% of all those surveyed reported 
plans to sell. However, only 23% of consumers reported 
owning an investment property – as a share of this sub-
group, the proportion planning to sell is just over 26%. While 
it is unclear whether this is relatively high (no comparable 
measures are available from Westpac’s Home Ownership 
survey), a third of this same sub-group reported an intention to 
buy an investment property over the next year, indicating that 
amongst investment property owners prospective buyers still 
outnumber prospective sellers. 
Overall, the consumer views on housing show a clear tension. 
On the one side, high interest rates, a continued slide in prices 
and unease about a riskier outlook for jobs and the wider 
economy are weighing heavily on housing-related sentiment. 
But against this, relatively few are planning to sell and there 
is a relatively high proportion of consumers looking to buy. 
This suggests that conditions could recover quite quickly if and 
when the macro-economic backdrop becomes less threatening 
and the market starts to stabilise.

The survey is conducted by OZINFO & DYNATA. Respondents are selected at random. This latest survey is based on 1200 adults aged 18 years and over, across Australia. It was conducted in the week from 
2 November to 6 November.  The data have been weighted to reflect Australia’s population distribution. Copyright at all times remains with the Melbourne Institute of Applied Economic and Social Research.
About the Westpac Housing Sentiment Index 
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index is a composite 
measure based on a weighted combination of four indexes 
from the Westpac–Melbourne Institute Consumer 
Sentiment survey. Two of these are ‘primary’ components 
with a higher weight that relate directly to consumer 
perceptions of housing market conditions: the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute ‘time to buy a dwelling’ index and the 
Westpac–Melbourne Institute House Price Expectations 
Index. The remaining ‘supplementary’ components, with 
lower weights, relate to consumer assessments of job 
security – the Westpac–Melbourne Institute Unemployment 
Expectations Index – and risk appetite – the Westpac Risk 
Aversion Index.
Each of these components is seasonally adjusted, converted 
to a consistent base and combined using fixed weights 
determined by historical regression analysis. Note that the 
house price expectations component is only available from 
2009 on – a re-weighted composite based on the remaining 
measures is used for earlier periods.  
The resulting composite measures provide significant insight 
into housing market conditions both nationally and at the 
individual state level. The national index has over 50yrs of 
history and a clear lead indicator relationship with a variety 
of housing market metrics. The index is particularly good at 
picking turning points in housing market turnover – correctly 
anticipating every major upswing and downturn since 1980 
with a lead of around three months (four once the timeliness 
of sentiment updates is included).

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Share markets rallied across much of the globe. In the
US, reports of an upgraded outlook for CoreWeave, an
oversubscribed IPO for Anthropic and a stabilisation in
Nvidia supported renewed optimism around AI which

buoyed tech stocks, seeing the S&P 500 (+1.4%) and
Nasdaq (+1.7%) both rally, though the smaller gain for the
Dow Jones (+0.5%) shows the rally was more narrowly
based.
•  Across the pond, the Euro Stoxx 50 continued to build on
recent momentum with another solid gain (+1.0%), while
London’s FTSE 100 bounced back from a modest decline
yesterday (+0.8%). Stocks were up across most of Asia,
including Tokyo (+1.3%), Shanghai (+0.4%) and Seoul
(+2.6%), with only the Hang Seng (–1.0%) bucking the

wider trend. The ASX 200 rose 0.3% yesterday, but weaker
trading in futures overnight points to a mixed open this
morning.
•  Cash trading in bond markets were closed in the US for
Veterans Day, but trading in futures points to a continued
rise in yields following hotter-than-expected inflation
data received earlier in the week, which all but cemented
a December FOMC rate hike. Beyond December, markets
are also pricing in another two rate hikes over the course of

2027.
• Yields were little changed elsewhere, with the 10Y Bund
and Gilt steady on the day. The 10Y JGB meanwhile
continued to sell-off, with yields jumping 10bps to 2.80%
having effectively unwound the rally over late last week.
ACGBs were also weaker during local trading, seeing yields
rise 7bps on the 3Y and 6bps on the 10Y, following the
sharp rise in global yields the day prior post US CPI. Swaps
markets point to no hikes nor cuts from the RBA through to

end-2027 at present.
•  The USD received a solid bid overnight, buoyed by sharp
inflows into AI and tech stocks that improved risk sentiment.
The DXY finished 0.7% higher at around 102.9, the
strongest since April 2025. That move has pushed the Euro
down 0.6% back to a USD1.10 handle, the weakest since
May 2025, while the Yen slipped 0.7% to JPY159.9, the
weakest since the before the early-September rally. While
moves in the AUD was comparatively small overnight,

slipping 0.2% to around the USD0.6850 mark, that move
builds on recent downward momentum and has the currency
delicately positioned near 2026’s lows.
•  Oil prices rose slightly overnight, stabilising somewhat after
recent declines amid ongoing uncertainty around the future
of the US-Iran War, as President Trump downplays the
possibility of further strikes on Iran all while negotiations
show little progress. Brent rose 1.0% to US$92.4/bbl. The
rebound in risk sentiment buoyed metals, with LME copper

up 1.0% to US$13,817/mt while LME aluminium jumped
1.9% to US$3,068/mt. Gold prices meanwhile dipped 1.2%,
falling back below the US$4,000/oz mark, building on recent
momentum post US CPI which saw real yields jump higher
International Data
There was no major data out yesterday.
Domestic Data
In Australia, the value of dwelling finance approvals surprised
materially to the downside in Q3, the 15.5% decline marking
the largest quarterly decline on records going back to 1991.

The decline looks to be mostly volume-driven, with the number
of loans falling sharply compared to the average size of loans.
Across segments, investor lending values dropped by nearly a
third (see here).

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sovereign debt_ When Short-Term Shocks and Shifts in Debt Structure Leave a Lasting Fiscal Burden — The Case of Brazil (2026-11-12).pdf

Date: 2026-11-12

SOVEREIGN DEBT: WHEN SHORT-TERM SHOCKS AND SHIFTS IN DEBT STRUCTURE LEAVE A LASTING FISCAL
BURDEN — THE CASE OF BRAZIL
Salim Hammad
Brazil’s recent post-election yield shock risks leaving a lasting imprint on public finances — compounded by already high interest rates and a
changing debt structure. Large refinancing needs and a rising share of floating-rate debt will transmit rapidly and significantly higher interest

rates to the debt service through distinct but reinforcing channels.
SOURCES : MACROBOND, BCB

An abrupt post-election market awakening

In the wake of the 2 nd round of the general elections in late October, Brazilian markets — which had expected a Flavio Bolsonaro
victory — sold off sharply after Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva won a fourth presidential term. Both the equity and FX markets
experienced large intraday drops (14% of the Ibovespa stock index and -11% BRL vs. USD). Meanwhile, the yield on Brazil’s 10-year
government bond rose 250 basis points to 15.5%, reaching its highest level in over a decade (Chart 1) as investors worried about

the fiscal impact of costly campaign promises amidst large and persistent fiscal imbalances.
Fiscal risks have reshaped Brazil’s public debt structure, magnifying the cost of persistently high interest rates
Absent a confidence-infusing fiscal adjustment, Brazil’s interest bill is unlikely to decline in the short term, maintaining upward
pressure on public debt (83% of GDP in October 2026) and limiting budgetary leeway.
Three transmission channels, amplified by changing features of the debt structure, explain this persistence.

The first channel: The rise in long-term yields will increase rollover costs.
The sovereign must refinance roughly 20% of its debt stock each year. An average maturity of 4.1 years spreads refinancing over
time but does not shield the interest bill from a sustained rise in borrowing costs. As maturing securities are replaced, higher
issuance rates progressively lift the average borrowing cost — even for fixed-rate debt, whose interest costs remain unchanged

until refinancing. A sustained yield shock therefore feeds into debt servicing as the stock rolls over.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 10 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The second channel: Demand for floating-rate instruments will strengthen until confidence in fiscal policy improves.
In turn, a larger share of SELIC-linked debt heightens the debt stock’s sensitivity to the prevailing high policy rate. Already SELIC-linked
securities reached a record 52.6% of the debt stock in August 2026, around 12 percentage points above their share at the start of President

Lula’s last term, versus a low of 18.4% in September 2014 (Chart 2). Weaker demand for fixed-rate securities with maturities exceeding five
years, amid increased fiscal uncertainty, has encouraged this shift 1.
The third channel: The BCB may delay rate cuts until fiscal policy over the next mandate is better defined.
A slower easing cycle would prolong high interest costs on SELIC-linked debt, whose servicing costs adjust with the policy rate without

waiting for refinancing. With these securities accounting for a growing share of the debt stock, delayed monetary easing would postpone
relief for the Treasury’s interest bill — even if long-term market yields subsequently ease.
These channels operate at different speeds: a rise in long-term yields increases rollover costs, whereas floating-rate debt reprices with
the SELIC rate, not automatically with 10-year yields. Meanwhile, a slower monetary easing cycle and stronger demand for floating-rate

securities compound pressure on the interest bill.
The fiscal–monetary policy nexus is therefore likely to remain a source of strain on Brazil’s public finances: fiscal uncertainty could delay
monetary easing, while greater floating-rate exposure would prolong the burden of high policy rates on the interest bill.
salim.hammad@bnpparibas.com
1 Fiscal slippage and uncertainty make investors less willing to lock in nominal returns over long horizons: if inflation or market yields rise, the purchasing power of fixed payments

declines or the bonds’ market value falls. Investors therefore demand higher yields to bear this risk, making long-term fixed-rate funding more expensive for the Treasury. By issuing
floating-rate bonds instead, the Treasury offers returns that adjust with the SELIC rate, sustaining demand, securing financing and reducing the risk of failed auctions. However
longer floating-rate maturities may reduce rollover risk, but do not remove the repricing exposure. Floating-rate issuance helps secure financing but transfers interest-rate risk to

the Treasury.
SOURCES : NATIONAL TREASURY, BNP PARIBAS

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
US-China talks, a possible Trump-Xi-Putin trilateral meeting, and any signs of a China-Japan diplomatic
thaw will be critical watchpoints. The priorities of the summit between representatives of 21 Asia-Pacific
economies are set to include efforts to boost innovation, digital connectivity, supply-chain resilience and

support for an open multilateral trading system. During the summit, presidents Trump and Xi are expected to
meet for the third time this year. This meeting will provide signals on the state of China-US talks, including the
operationalisation of new bilateral trade and investment boards, and on the likelihood of another extension of
the Busan trade truce beyond January 2027. President Trump has also indicated he is open to a trilateral US-
China-Russia meeting during the summit, possibly covering nuclear arms control and the Russia-Ukraine war.

Investors will also closely watch whether Japanese Prime Minister Takaichi and President Xi meet on the
sidelines, following reports that Tokyo is hoping to arrange a meeting. However, the bar for a formal
breakthrough will likely remain high. As we noted in a report earlier this year, a full normalisation of relations
and an easing of Chinese economic restrictions – which include rare-earth export controls – may require a
lengthy period of diplomatic re-engagement and backchannel diplomacy.

A divided government could mean less change at home but more abroad. Third-party polling and betting
markets forecast Democrat control of the House, and an increasing probability of flipping the Senate. 80% of
respondents in our monthly poll now say living costs will influence their vote – the most since April. For
markets, a divided government could mean less domestic legislative change, but not necessarily a more
predictable US foreign policy. Losing Congress could leave theadministration’s geopolitical and geoeconomic

toolkit largely intact.
In our view, weapons stockpiles, inflationary pressures and long-end yields may matter more than Congress
in constraining theadministration’s choices in 2027 and 2028. A Democratic House could dial up oversight of
the administration, launch investigations, force votes on War Powers Resolutions, and use appropriations bills
to press for restrictions on departmental funding. This may generate a steady stream of attention-grabbing

headlines, but whether it decisively affects the event-driven risk that matters for markets (e.g. blocking a tariff
or preventing a limited military strike) remains in doubt. If domestic policymaking becomes more challenging,
foreign policy could become a top priority for the Trump administration.
In the spotlight for November 2026: US midterms and APEC Summit
US midterm elections (3 November)
APEC Economic Leaders’ Meeting in Shenzhen (18-19 November)

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
NewZealand’s general election is shaping up as a close and fragmented contest. Both the ruling National
and opposition Labour parties are polling at historically low levels, and smaller parties will likely determine
the next government. In a closely contested vote, investors could see coalition uncertainty and a wider

range of possible outcomes for tax, spending, infrastructure, energy and migration policy.
The National party is campaigning on a platform that would broadly preserve the current emphasis on
fiscal restraint, lower regulation and private-sector investment. A Labour-led government could place
greater weight on public services, housing and climate policy, alongside a proposed capital-gains tax on
investment property. However, because any government will rely on minor parties for survival, the final

policy mix will depend heavily on post-election negotiations.
The meeting will be a test of the EU’s ability to translate the competitiveness agenda into practical
reforms. The focus will be on Europe’s ability to restore competitiveness, mobilize investment and
maintain fiscal discipline. The Eurogroup brings together Euro-area finance ministers and is expected to
consider macroeconomic and fiscal developments, energy market pressures and progress towards the

Savings and Investments Union. ECOFIN brings together all EU finance ministers, and will focus on
financial integration, banking sector competitiveness, and economic coordination. To watch will be
tensions between fiscal consolidation and the need to fund defence, energy security and strategic
investment, alongside progress on integrating Europe’s fragmented capital markets and banking sectors. A
budget-focused Council meeting follows on 10 November.

Implementation of existing commitments, rather than new policy pledges, is expected to be the primary
focus of the next climate summit in Antalya. Building on the shift towards implementation at COP30,
COP31 will focus on translating existing climate commitments into practical action, including through
investment, financing and partnerships. For investors, the key watchpoint will be whether the summit can
deliver tangible commitments that translate existing plans into binding funding pledges for critical

infrastructure resilience, scaled-up renewable energy output, and climate finance.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order
Eurogroup and Economic and Financial Affairs Council (ECOFIN) meetings (9 November)
Source: Jill Beckmann on Unsplash
New Zealand general election (7 November)
COP 31 in Türkiye (9-20 November)

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
Melaka’s state election will offer a further gauge of Malaysia’s evolving electoral landscape after
strong recent state-level performances by Barisan Nasional (BN). The contest will also be watched for
its read-through to Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim’s Pakatan Harapan (PH), following setbacks in other

state elections this year.
BN enters the election from a position of relative strength, while its electoral understanding with PAS
and its allies could provide an early indication of how political alignments are evolving ahead of the next
general election. However, Melaka’s local dynamics mean the result should not be treated as a direct
national forecast.
The anticipated signing of a landmark digital economy agreement is set to be one of the most

significant summit deliverables in years. The 49th ASEAN Summit in Manila will focus on economic
integration, energy security, supply-chain resilience, maritime security, the crisis in Myanmar, and
relations with external partners.
For markets, the formal signing of the ASEAN Digital Economy Framework Agreement (DEFA) is the key
event to watch. Designed as a legally binding treaty, DEFA aims to harmonize regional digital trade rules
and cross-border data flows. Successful implementation is expected to double the projected size of the

region’s digital economy by 2030, boosting it from a projected baseline of US$1 trillion to US$2 trillion
(up from approximately US$300 billion today).
The second EU-UK Summit will be an important test of whether the post-Brexit relationship can move
towards tangible outcomes. Prime Minister Andy Burnham will seek to finalize agreements on food and
agricultural standards, youth mobility, and linking emissions trading systems. Burnham has framed
relations as“not good enough.” To watch will be whether we see concrete deals versus declarations, and

whether leaders are prepared to make the concessions necessary to deepen ties.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order
49th ASEAN Summit (16-18 November)
Source: istockphoto
EU-UK Summit (20 November)
Melaka state election (14 November)

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
The upcoming local elections could serve as a political barometer ahead of the 2028 general election–
but only up to a point. Local elections in Taiwan are primarily driven by municipal ‘bread and butter’
economic issues; geopolitics and cross-Strait relations have historically not been at the forefront of

voters’ concerns. While this divergence limits theelections’ predictive value for the January 2028 general
vote, the results can provide a sense of the domestic balance of power.
Elections in Taiwan’s six special municipalities – Taipei, New Taipei, Taichung, Tainan, Taoyuan, and
Kaohsiung – will serve as particularly important watchpoints. Some recent polling suggests a potentially
close contest in Taipei between incumbent mayor Chiang Wan-an (Kuomintang) and legislator Shen Po-

yang (Democratic Progressive Party).
Spain’s snap general election could mark a major turning point for Spanish politics and the EU,
potentially ending eight years of Socialist-led government under Pedro Sánchez and bringing the
conservative People’s Party (PP) to power with support from the far-right Vox. Polling suggests PP is
ahead and a PP-Vox alignment is the only one projected to reach a majority. A rightward shift in one of
the EU’s largest economies could occur just as Europe enters a wider electoral cycle, potentially

complicating consensus on migration, climate policy, Ukraine and European integration.
The Forum comes at a pivotal moment in EU-Latin American relations. It is the EU’s first big commercial
push into Argentina since the EU-Mercosur trade deal began to be provisionally applied on 1 May. The
agreement opens new opportunities for trade, investment and market access. The EU is Argentina’s
largest source of foreign direct investment. Around 800 government, business and financial leaders will

focus on energy, critical minerals, agriculture and infrastructure. The EU’s Global Gateway investment
agenda provides a framework for mobilizing public and private capital. While a key outcome will be the
launch of a structured EU-Argentina investment dialogue, to watch will be the ability of Argentine
officials to allay concerns around policy stability and regulatory predictability.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order

Source: Nestor Barbitta on Unsplash
EU-Argentina Investment and Business Forum (30 November – 1 December)
Spain general election (29 November)
Taiwan local elections (28 November)

### Page 7

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
A potential Trump-Kim meeting in November could restart official bilateral dialogue, though North
Korea remains highly unlikely to abandon its nuclear program. South Korea’s National Intelligence
Service said in late September that it saw a roughly 50% chance of US-North Korea talks around the
APEC Summit in Shenzhen, although more recent reporting suggests the prospects may have

diminished. If talks do resume, they would only mark the first step in a long and highly uncertain
diplomatic process.
As we noted earlier this year, North Korea is highly unlikely to agree to denuclearization. Unless renewed
negotiations sidestep denuclearization in favor of a risk-reduction or coexistence framework, dialogue is
likely to stall, not least given Pyongyang’s constitutional pledge to continue expanding its nuclear
arsenal. This would make even a negotiated freeze on further expansion difficult to achieve.

Document 425: Time To Sound The All Clear Signal_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Time To Sound The All Clear Signal_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Time To Sound The All Clear Signal?
Date:    6 hours ago
Time To Sound The All Clear Signal?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Time To Sound The All Clear
Signal?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 11 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Guille Álvarez / Unsplash

I. From Caution to Buying Opportunities
On September 15, we advised our readers to "Proceed With Caution." In
that note, we pushed our year-end S&P 500 target of 8,400 to mid-2027
and lowered our 2026 target to 7,900. Our concern wasn't earnings. We
maintained our 2027 S&P 500 EPS forecast of $425 but lowered our year-
end forward P/E forecast from 19.8 to 18.6.

### Page 2

Why? Our worry list was getting longer: geopolitical conflicts, rising bond
yields, domestic political uncertainty, and concerns about the AI trade. We
reckoned that these risks were increasing and would depress valuations
(FOMO) despite the ongoing strength of the economy and fabulous
earnings momentum (FEMO).
We reiterated our caution on October 14. On October 24, we observed
that buying opportunities were starting to emerge, but better ones were

likely after the midterms. On November 2, we warned that a Democratic
sweep could deliver a cold shower for Wall Street. The Nasdaq plunged
on November 5. That evening's QT was titled, "We Promised You Buying
Opportunities After The Midterms." We concluded that Thursday's selloff
was the buying opportunity we had anticipated.
Our post-election cold shower warning proved timely. After initially rallying
on Wednesday following Tuesday's midterm elections on November 3, the

S&P 500 sold off sharply on Thursday. It found support at its 200-day
moving average and has rebounded since then (chart). It is only 2.6%
below its October 5 record high. The S&P 500 equal weight index is back
at its record high.
The Nasdaq rose to a record high on October 6 (chart). It then plunged
11.9% into correction territory through November 5. It is up 4.7% since
then.

### Page 3

FEMO has offset FOMO. Soaring S&P 500 forward earnings has offset
the drop in the forward P/E (charts). We think that the latter probably
bottomed on November 5, while the former should continue rising, albeit
at a slower pace.

### Page 4

Our worry list is getting shorter:
(1) Geopolitics. The feared post-midterm re-escalation of the Middle East
war did not materialize. Instead, President Donald Trump adopted a more
conciliatory tone after Republican election losses, which some
Republicans partly blamed on the unpopular war. Correspondingly, oil
prices have retreated recently (chart). The White House now seems
determined to maintain the status quo by continuing to blockade Iran,
while seeking a diplomatic solution.

(2) Bond yield. The 10-year Treasury yield is back in our old normal
range of 4.00%-5.00% (charts). We view this range as consistent with
solid economic growth. It rebounded to the top of the range yesterday
after October's hotter-than-expected CPI, which increased the odds of a

### Page 5

25bps Fed rate hike early next month. We've expected a 50bps hike ever
since the last FOMC meeting, which voted against another hike.
(3) Domestic policies. The midterms are done, removing one key source
of uncertainty. The market's recent rebound suggests investors are
adjusting to the new political landscape, though fiscal standoffs over the
federal budget and debt ceiling remain a risk. In our opinion, the Blue
Wave in Congress and state governorships might slow data center

construction, but it won't stop the AI buildout (chart).

### Page 6

(4) AI investment boom. Investors seem to be coming around to our view
that financial, political, and physical constraints should be speed bumps,
not roadblocks, to the AI buildout. That's principally because demand for
computing power remains exceptionally strong. CoreWeave's earnings
report yesterday provides further evidence, with Q3 revenue surging
168% y/y to $3.7 billion and backlog reaching $132.5 billion. Such robust
demand not only justifies continued investment in computing capacity but

also helps ease financing concerns. Indeed, the company expects roughly
$9 billion in customer prepayments and deposits, reducing its reliance on
expensive borrowing.
But is this exceptionally strong demand for computing power sustainable?
The latest results from OpenAI and Anthropic suggest that it is. OpenAI's
Q3 revenue surged 160% y/y to $10 billion, while Anthropic's soared
1,127% to almost $17 billion. This exponential revenue growth provides

compelling evidence of rapidly expanding demand for AI services, which
will continue to fuel compute demand. Meanwhile, reports that Anthropic's
IPO is already multiple times oversubscribed suggest that investors
remain eager to finance the boom.
II. Bearish Sentiment Is Bullish
We are pleased to see that the Investors Intelligence Bull/Bear Ratio
plunged to 1.54 last week (chart). We expected that to happen in
response to last Thursday's panic attack. From a contrarian perspective,

this is a strong buy signal.

### Page 7

For the remainder of the year, we expect analysts to moderate their
earnings forecasts as they account for the speed bumps ahead, while
investors become more comfortable paying a higher multiple as earnings
estimates become more credible and sentiment improves.

Document 431: Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade

Source PDF: all report pdf/Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Global trade will remain resilient in 2027, but the focus will
shift from tariﬀs to ownership, technology and investment.
While not our base case, an escalation in an EU-China trade
war would rank among the most signiﬁcant risks to global
trade

Inga Fechner and Carsten Brzeski
Europe is undoubtedly facing a second 'China shock' – only this time around,
it's largely negative
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Trade survives politics
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

Global goods trade continues to weather geopolitical shocks.
Over the past few years, trade has proved far more resilient
than global headlines suggest. We expect that resilience to
continue into 2027. Disruptions in global shipping, shifting
tariﬀ regimes and recurring geopolitical tensions have
repeatedly altered trade routes without derailing trade itself.
Strong demand for AI-related investment and infrastructure
should continue to support trade ﬂows, particularly across

Asia, while selective US-China decoupling is likely to beneﬁt
third countries that attract production and investment. For
2027, we expect global goods trade to remain positive,
expanding about 3% year-on-year. In short, trade survives
politics in 2027 because companies can still adapt faster
than governments can restrict them.
Our risky call: Ownership matters more than
trade
Tariﬀs were only the ﬁrst step. By 2027, the real battleground shifts
to capital, technology and ownership. As companies invest behind

tariﬀ walls, governments stop merely screening new investment
and begin requiring companies to restructure, separate sensitive
operations or divest existing strategic assets.
A foreign-owned battery plant, semiconductor facility, or data
centre once supported for bringing local investment and jobs is
now treated as a national-security risk. Companies that once
navigated geopolitical tensions through diversiﬁcation ﬁnd
themselves forced to choose sides. The question is no longer who

exports the most, but who controls the productive capacity on
which economies depend. Trade would continue, but the corporate
structures that have kept it moving would start to fracture as
governments alter trade ﬂows. This could potentially cause global
goods trade growth to stall or even contract in 2027.
Our bold call: EU-China trade war

irrespective of a particular user's means, ﬁnancial situation or investment

Read more
Download
Included in the following bundle
The EU and China enter an outright trade conﬂict. Europe abandons

its largely open-market approach and increasingly applies
reciprocity principles to market access and investment. Chinese
ﬁrms investing in strategic sectors face mandatory 50:50 joint
ventures, local ownership requirements and growing restrictions on
acquisitions.
At the same time, the EU imposes broad tariﬀs on Chinese imports,
with rates reaching 50% in politically sensitive sectors such as
vehicles, chemicals, machinery and electronics. China retaliates

with measures of similar scale.
The consequences are signiﬁcant. China accounts for roughly 15%
of global goods trade, while the EU accounts for around 13.5%.
Together they represent almost 30% of world goods trade. With the
EU being China's largest trading partner in goods and China being
the EU's second-largest trading partner in goods, a full-scale trade
conﬂict would rank among the most consequential economic
conﬂicts of the modern era.

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
206 收藏 分享到 :
习近平强调，当前国际形势变乱交织，中韩都是多边主义和⾃由贸易的受益者。双⽅要进
⼀步加强多边协作，共同践⾏真正的多边主义，推进亚太共同体和亚太⾃贸区建设，为地
区和平与发展作出更⼤贡献，维护全球产业链供应链稳定畅通，推动构建更加公正合理的
全球治理体系。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 18 ⽇上午，国家主席习近平在深圳会⻅来华出席亚太经合组织领导⼈⾮正式会议的韩国总
统李在明。
习近平指出，中韩是友好近邻和战略合作伙伴。两国国情不同，但为⼈⺠谋福祉的⽬标是相通
的。明年是中韩建交 35 周年，双⽅应以此为契机，总结经验、规划未来，推动中韩关系稳健发
展，更好造福两国⼈⺠。双⽅要坚持战略⾃主，维护好两国关系政治基础，巩固好、发展好中
韩关系。两国政党、政府、⽴法机构可以加强治国理政经验交流，增进相互理解，促进共同发

展。中韩实质性完成了⾃贸协定第⼆阶段谈判，有助于巩固互利共赢良好局⾯。双⽅要⽤好⽤
⾜这⼀制度性安排，加强宏观政策协调，培育⼈⼯智能等新兴科技产业合作⽣态，共享发展机
遇。中⽅愿同韩⽅⼀道，持续促进两国⼈员往来，循序渐进开展健康有益的⽂化交流。
习近平强调，当前国际形势变乱交织，中韩都是多边主义和⾃由贸易的受益者。双⽅要进⼀步
加强多边协作，共同践⾏真正的多边主义，推进亚太共同体和亚太⾃贸区建设，为地区和平与
发展作出更⼤贡献，维护全球产业链供应链稳定畅通，推动构建更加公正合理的全球治理体
系。
李在明表⽰，在我和习近平主席战略引领下，韩中关系全⾯恢复势头不断巩固，双边经贸合作
快速增⻓，⼈员往来密切，相互认知不断改善，为两国⼈⺠带来实实在在的福祉。韩⽅坚持⼀
个中国的政策没有也不会改变，愿以明年两国建交 35 周年为契机，同中⽅密切⾼层交往，加强
贸易、投资、产供链等合作，促进⺠⼼相通，推动建设更成熟的韩中战略伙伴关系。韩⽅祝贺
并⽀持中⽅成功担任亚太经合组织东道主，愿同中⽅携⼿合作，建设共同繁荣的亚太共同体。

蔡奇、王毅、⻩坤明等参加会⻅。

Document 617: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_6949 沛爾生醫_陳宣甫_20261111

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_6949 沛爾生醫_陳宣甫_20261111.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

法說會重點摘要

沛爾生醫 
(6949 TT, NT$44.75, 未評等)

重點摘要 
1. PL001（CD19 CAR-T）第二期臨床試驗於 4M26 因已達統計上顯著
意義而提前終止，並經獨立數據監測委員會（IDMC）確認；一期
至二期共收案 24 例、本次公開 21 例，對比諾華 Kymriah 的 P 值為
0.008，反應持續時間（DOR）10.5 個月、無惡化存活期（PFS）已
自 11.3 個月延長至 14 個月，最高等級細胞激素釋放症候群
（CRS）與神經系統副作用均未發生。公司現與 CDE、TFDA 組成的
T-RMAT 團隊共同備件，為台灣首件再生醫療製劑查驗登記案 
2. 子公司台灣細胞製造（tcmc）竹北廠產能 300 例/年、製程 7 天，
相較 Kymriah 在台採血送瑞士、Yescarta 送洛杉磯可省去跨境運送
成本。我們認為在地製造與自製慢病毒（LV）是 PL001 的核心優
勢，以兩者在台健保 819 萬元/劑為基準，取證後公司在高毛利與低
價搶占給付之間具雙向彈性 
3. PP021 憑藉一期臨床與海外真實人體數據的安全性，在美國《嘗試
權法案（Right to Try ）》通過不久即獲美方主動邀請，創下尚未取
得正式藥證便合法落地美國、提前自費開賣的罕見紀錄；這不僅打
破了生技研發漫長燒錢的宿命、為公司帶進早期實質現金流，更在
美國本土實戰累積關鍵真實世界數據（RWE），為 2027 年進軍帕
金森氏症與阿茲海默症二期樞紐試驗及未來的全球授權談判，奠定
了戰略主導權。 
結論 
我們認為投資評估重心已從臨床數據轉向定價與商業化執行。PL001
療效與 Kite Pharma 產品無統計差異，但公司收治的是二線治療失敗、
病況較差的病人，第三期將把收案拉到第二線，我們預期數據會比原
廠其他藥更有競爭力。近期驗證點為 T-RMAT 送件與取證時程、tcmc
的 CDMO 訂單承接，以及 PP021 第一期於 12M26 結束後的數據揭
露，及 Right to try 法規路徑的突破性商轉結果

PL001 二期數據與查驗登記進度 
 第二期臨床試驗於 4M26 通過期中分析，因已達統計上顯著意義，
基於醫學倫理提前終止，所有委員均同意；2026/4/10 通過獨立數
據監測委員會（IDMC）的資料安全審查 
 一期至二期共收案 24 例，本次公開分析對象為 21 例；客觀療效 P
值對比諾華 Kymriah 為 0.008 幾，小於 0.05，數據由獨立委員會
計算而非公司自行統計 
 反應持續時間（DOR）至 4M26 為 10.5 個月，Kite Pharma 發表
為 11.1 個月；公司資料截止後現已超過 11 個月，多數病人仍維
持腫瘤消失狀態，諾華並未公告其療效維持期間

凱基投顧

陳宣甫 
886.2.2181.8724 
hsuan.chen@kgi.com

徐雋翔

### Page 2

台灣 沛爾生醫

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

 無惡化存活期（PFS）為 11.3 個月無變化，資料截止後已延長至
14 個月；吉利德（Gilead）產品為 5.8 個月 
 最高等級細胞激素釋放症候群（CRS）與神經系統副作用均未發
生；臨床上約 2 週內出現 CRS、其後 2-3 天須觀察神經系統反應 
 與 10M26 通過健保的 Kite Pharma 產品相比療效無優劣差異，但
對方選入的病人條件較佳，公司收治的是二線治療失敗者；第三期
將比照把早期病人納入收案 
 現與藥品查驗中心（CDE）及食品藥物管理署（TFDA）組成的 T-
RMAT 團隊共同備件，公司為台灣第一案；第一步申請即需數千頁
資料並分批遞交，公司表示資料送進官方後進度無法控制 
 台灣目前僅 8 家醫學中心可收治 CAR-T 病人，主管機關顧慮醫院開
得了但照顧不來，公司正爭取放寬

製造優勢與定價空間 
 子公司台灣細胞製造（tcmc）位於竹北生醫園區、持股 66.88%，
CAR-T 產能規劃 300 例/年 
 諾華 Kymriah 在台採血後送瑞士、Kite Pharma Yescarta 送洛杉
磯，公司在地製造可省去這一趟的產能風險與費用；台大醫院送台
南、高雄的交期都很快 
 慢病毒（LV）是除人工以外成本最高的環節，以往在 819 萬元/劑
的售價下單一病人可賺 100 萬元、後降至 50 萬元，公司自製後有
能力再把毛利往上拉 
 台灣方面諾華 Kymriah 已有健保給付，Kite Pharma Yescarta 於
10M26 通過健保，同為 819 萬元/劑 
 公司認為可優於競品的原因有二：在地製造；DNA 設計與競品不
同，未踩到對方專利且該專利已到期

技術平台與實體癌布局 
 傳統單鏈 CAR-T 的細胞結合力與毒殺效率受限，業界多從彈頭（抗
原辨識區）改良，公司認為改彈頭並不足夠，改的是鏈數：彈頭不
變，把單鏈改為三條鏈、三個蛋白 
 PL002 以間皮素（mesothelin）為彈頭，卵巢癌動物試驗中多鏈
CAR-T 打完約 10 天腫瘤明顯縮小、第 40 天完全消失；傳統單鏈僅
部分有效、腫瘤仍存在，一般 T 細胞組第 40 天仍整體長大 
 先做卵巢癌是因為半數以上病人在 2-3 年內復發、可選標靶僅一
個，且病人多在 40-60 歲；間皮素在胰臟癌與卵巢癌表現率達
70%以上，成果可沿用至胰臟癌，為下一階段標的 
 自體 CAR-T 無排斥與移植物抗宿主疾病（GvHD）風險、可在體內
長期存活，是目前療效的天花板：原本 6 個月內死亡的病人打完
CAR-T 後 5 年有半數以上存活；缺點是無法大批次生產、價格高且
需冷鏈運送

### Page 3

台灣 沛爾生醫

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

 公司前後約 2 年投入的異體 CAR-T 抑制抗體開發已停止，現全部轉
向 in vivo CAR -T，手上有兩個版本正在比較強弱；該技術專利尚未
取得，2027 年才會完整說明

小肽平台與美國 Right to Try 路徑 
 小肽來源為 17 年前自兔皮萃取物經高效液相層析（HPLC）分出的
6 段蛋白之一，口服 30 分鐘可穿透血腦屏障（BBB）並在腦組織與
腦脊髓液測到；臨床試驗用 GMP 等級藥物已製成，歐盟已有 2 個
國家索取、擬把自家藥物接在小肽下帶進腦部，產出的智慧財產共
享 
 帕金森氏症動物試驗中，爬竿時間、步態評分、握力與顫抖評分均
呈劑量效應，效果與第一線用藥相近，酪胺酸羥化酶（TH）染色顯
示可保護黑質多巴胺神經元；阿茲海默症的築巢與水迷宮試驗結果
相同 
 公司主張現行帕金森氏症療法為補充療法，治療期間多巴胺神經元
仍持續死亡，殘存細胞耗竭後只能植入深腦刺激器（DBS、自費約
70-80 萬元），公司藥物的差異在於保護住健康神經 
 PP021 第一期臨床預計 12M26 結束；帕金森氏症第二期預計 2Q27
於北京天壇醫院啟動，4Q27 前進入正式第二期，試驗計畫書已大
致完成並將先在台灣推進 
 美國路徑走 Right to Try ：公司已接到邀請、2027 年進入美國，美
方正建立平台並提供自由貿易區（free trade zone）處理 100%關
稅，且已找到醫院願意開方；台灣沒有這項制度，故台灣、中國、
歐盟仍走正規臨床路線 
 第二代產品把小肽環化，半衰期（half-life）更長、一天一次即可
（現為一天 2-3 次），第二個專利年限 20-25 年；現有專利還有 17
年 
 疾病人數與市場：帕金森氏症美國 100 萬人、中國 500 萬人、歐
盟 200 多萬人，阿茲海默症中國 1,000 多萬人、美國 700-800 萬
人；僅美國市場帕金森氏症約 70 億美元、阿茲海默症約 150 億美
元、漸凍症約 10 億美元 
 Right to try: 《Right to Try 》繞過冗長審批，直接在美國本土落地
用藥；傳統美墨或境外知情同意用藥雖然能累積數據，但要正式進
入美國病患端，通常得等漫長的 IND/Phase 1/2 。而美國聯邦《嘗
試權法案（Right to Try Act ）》允許已完成 Phase 1 臨床安全性驗
證、仍在進行中的新藥，在未獲 FDA 正式核准前，經醫師與病患
簽署後合法自費使用。這讓 PP021 能在 2027 年臨床推進的同時，
搶先在美國建立真實世界的病患群體與現金流。公司已接到邀請、
2027 年進入美國，美方正建立平台並提供自由貿易區（free trade 
zone）處理 100%關稅，且已找到醫院願意開方；台灣沒有這項制
度，故台灣、中國、歐盟仍走正規臨床路線

### Page 4

台灣 沛爾生醫

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 4

海外市場與集團架構 
 公司規劃 2027 年進行組織架構重整，2027 年後集團架構變成兩岸
三地加美國，美國納斯達克與上海均另有考量；tcmc 預計 2027 年
上市 
 馬來西亞與泰國主動接洽，公司 11/29 赴當地洽技術移轉並協助建
廠；兩地目前均無 CAR-T，連中國的 CAR-T 也未進入 
 日本已簽保密協議（NDA）：當地一劑 3,240 萬日圓，美國業者做
不來並施壓厚生省，現行價格難以維持；日本 CD19 市場 420 多億
日圓、含 BCMA CAR-T 與多發性骨髓瘤合計 1,400 億日圓，且日
本無自有產品、民族性對基因改造排斥 
 小肽預計 2027 年初、不超過 2Q27 進美國，美方比公司更積極且
無關稅；此制度為專案進口，台灣尚無，美國、英國、香港均有並
可自費 
 中國市場公司仍在考慮，產品已被認定為第一類新藥、屬生醫十五
五規劃六大重點之一；優惠包括臨床試驗輔助、審查期 30 天內未
完成視同自動通過、取證當日自動進入醫保且不議價由廠商開價，
中國適應症人數 1,200 萬人 
 一家與公司有合作關係的公司以 13 億元買下新加坡一家公司並邀
請公司進入東南亞；東南亞除新加坡外均無 CAR-T ，印度 Tata 
Group 雖在孟買的醫院自行生產，公司認為不能算真正的 CAR-T

QA 
 小肽藥物原本用在類風濕性關節炎（RA），與帕金森氏症的機轉類
似嗎？ 
 這個藥對自體免疫疾病比較有效，英國也試過幾個，但 RA 第一
線到第三線的藥很多，沒有必要現在去競爭；戰略上先走帕金森
氏症與阿茲海默症，利用可過血腦屏障（BBB）的優勢 
 漸凍症也可以做、北榮正在洽談，但台灣只有 800-1,000 人、規
模太小不好做；若能通過，第二期即可發藥證，因屬罕見疾病 
 兩者的作用機制（MOA）是兩條不同的路；公司挑勝算最大、
市場最迫切需求的適應症先做 
 PL001 的療效比 Yescarta 等競爭對手更好嗎？ 
 跟 Yescarta 差不多，統計學上沒有差異，客觀緩解率與無惡化存
活期（PFS）都一樣 
 但對方是第一線用完失敗後才用，公司收的是第三線、二線失敗
的病人，病人條件比對方差；第三期會盡量拉到第二線 
 CAR-T 前往美國，冷鏈與運輸時間在成本上會不會有落差？ 
 從台灣運到美國一劑大概增加 20 萬元新台幣；公司 7 天製程可
以撐到 8 天，運到中國再加 2 天 
 UPS 已經把流程算好，早上 6 點多華航班機、UPS 接本地派送
（Local Domestic ）送達，對方認為划得來；美國人工成本高，
組織架構與成本仍要精算

### Page 5

台灣 沛爾生醫

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

 CAR-T 是美方主動要求的，公司本來只打算談小肽，對方是因為
現有產品太貴、公司可以省一半價格才主動詢問 
 台灣的審查進度有沒有影響到公司的合作？ 
 任何東西只要送到官方手中就無法控制；現在是官方與公司一起
合作要提 RMAT，公司是第一案，CDE 與 TFDA 合成一個小組與
公司開會 
 送件資料非常多，第一步申請就要幾千頁，且分批遞交；官方因
為是第一個案子也有面子工程的壓力 
 南韓在 4M26 發了 Curocell 的條件式核准（conditional 
approval），公司看過對方公開數據認為比公司差一級，所以對
自家送審有信心 
 以後是要自己做，還是中間有可能授權？ 
 本來是要授權的，原來的 offer 數字不小；後來考慮授權出去就
什麼都沒了、以後不能再碰，所以決定自己走 
 公司現在還有錢，走多久算多久；已有大咖要進來但一直擋著，
因為不缺錢且越往後做價值越高 
 台灣先取證之後，同時做第三期的話在台灣就可以先銷售嗎？ 
 可以。第三期要 5 年才能完成、日本是 6 年，所以計畫把日本、
馬來西亞、泰國納進來做第三期的多中心（Multi-Center）試驗 
 已經算過大概 110 到 120 個病例，試驗計畫書與費用都估得差
不多；公司算過現金還夠支撐，至於會不會被逼到賣掉，公司表
示盡量不要 
 PP021 的第一期資料大概什麼時候會出來？ 
 公司表示資料絕對沒問題，但要等中國國家藥品監督管理局
（NMPA）核准後才會公告 
 目前看到的不良事件都與藥物無關，主持人（PI）與友誼醫院的
醫師都寫 unrelated；官方要求只要出現問題就要寫，由試驗方
判斷關聯性、官方再判斷一次

### Page 3

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年10月
◆現状：100ドルを挟んで乱高下
９月のＷＴＩ原油先物価格は、月半ばにかけて
100ドル台後半に上昇。米国とイランの間で攻撃の
応酬が続いたことが背景。加えて、イエメンの武装組
織フーシがバブ・エル・マンデブ海峡付近の港湾都市
を掌握したことや、サウジアラビアの東西パイプライン
が攻撃を受けて操業を停止したことで、ホルムズ海
峡を迂回するルートからの石油輸出が停滞するとの
懸念も価格を押し上げ。月末にかけては、一時80ド
ル台後半に下落。米国とイランの外交交渉が進展
するとの期待や、東西パイプラインの輸送能力が早
期に回復するとの報道を材料に、中東からの石油供
給の増加が意識されたことが背景。
10月入り後は、90ドル前後で方向感に欠ける展
開。米国の中東への増派が価格を押し上げた一方、
Ｇ７による石油備蓄放出の合意が価格を下押し。
◆投機筋のネットポジションは小幅に拡大
投機筋の買い越し幅は緩やかに拡大。中東情勢
の緊迫化を受けて、原油価格の上昇を見込む投資

家が増加している可能性。
◆見通し：当面100ドル前後で高止まり
両国の戦闘がこれ以上激化しないことを前提とす
るメインシナリオでは、原油価格が100ドルを中心と
する高値圏で推移する見通し。ホルムズ海峡の通
航制約が価格を下支えする一方、東西パイプライン
などの代替ルートの活用や、中東産油国の輸出の
回復が上値を抑えることが背景。

### Page 4

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年10月3
◆長期化するホルムズ海峡の「封鎖」
足元では、米国とイランの戦闘終結に向けた交渉
が難航しており、ホルムズ海峡の封鎖が継続。船舶
の位置情報を知らせるＡＩＳのデータに基づく推計
では、同海峡を通航するタンカーの数がほぼゼロに。
◆通常航行が停止しても、原油輸出は増加
もっとも、中東の原油輸出量は足元にかけて緩や
かに増加。海峡封鎖が続くなかでも輸出が回復して
いる背景として、次の２点が指摘可能。
第１に、迂回ルートの活用。サウジアラビアやアラブ
首長国連邦（ＵＡＥ）は、パイプラインを経由して
原油を海峡外のヤンブー港やフジャイラ港に輸送。
３月以降、これらの港からの輸出が増加。
第２に、タンカー同士の積み替え（ＳＴＳ）の活
用。報道などによると、ＡＩＳを切って海峡を通過
することで、原油を海峡外に運び出す船舶が存在。
こうした船舶から別の船舶に積み替えられたうえで、
原油が輸出されている模様。実際、大規模な迂回
ルートを持たないイラク・クウェートからの輸出や、フー

シの台頭でヤンブー港からの輸出が急減しているサウ
ジアラビアの輸出が、足元にかけて増加。
◆米国・イランの戦闘激化なら、輸出減少も
先行きも、中東の原油輸出は緩やかに回復する
見通し。ただし、輸出回復は限られたパイプラインや
ＡＩＳを停止した船舶による不安定な輸送に依存。
戦闘が激化し、こうした輸送手段が攻撃されれば、
輸出が再び減少するリスク。

Document 648: 壊れかけの半導体株

Source PDF: all report pdf/壊れかけの半導体株.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 11 月 6 日(金)

1/4 
壊れかけの半導体株 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・世界的にＡＩ・半導体株が急落している。ＳＯＸ指数は６月下旬に既往高値を付けた後、
徐々に水準を切り下げ、米中間選挙を通過した後に一段と下落した。この間、日本の代表的
な半導体銘柄も同様の展開となっている。そして11月５日の米国市場では半導体株以外にも
下げが広がり、S&P500は4.4％、NASDAQは5.5％の下落となった。この約５ヶ月におけるＡ
Ｉ・半導体株の調整は、急上昇からの反動に加えて、①原油価格の高騰と、それを受けた②
主要中央銀行の利上げ、③米中間選挙で民主党が上下両院を奪還したことでデータセンター
建設を含めてＡＩインフラ投資が制約を受けるとの懸念があろう。

・①原油価格と②中央銀行の行動は密接に関係しており、これが年央以降の相場の重荷となっ
てきた。ここに来て、原油輸送の代替経路の確保、産油量の増加など原油価格を下押しする
要因もあるが、イラン情勢が根本的に解決する糸口はいまだ掴めておらず、ＷＴＩ原油価格
は、大きくみれば90～100ドル近辺で高止まりしている。それを受けてＦｅｄをはじめとする
主要中央銀行は夏場以降に利上げを進めており、９月には日銀、Ｆｅｄ、ＥＣＢが同時に利
上げを実施した。現時点では12月にも３中銀が同時利上げを実施するとの見方が多く、世界
の金融環境は引き締まる方向にある。なお、日銀の10月展望レポートでは「世界的にインフ
レ圧力が高まるなか、夏場以降、米欧を含む多くの中央銀行は利上げ方向に政策を転換して
・日経平均株価は先行き 12 ヶ月 72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPYは先行き 12ヶ月 155円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を 12月に 1.5％とした後、27年末までに 2.25％とするだろう 
・FEDはFF 金利を、年内は 4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/4 
おり、こうしたグローバルな金融環境の変化が、国際金融市場等を通じてわが国の経済・物
価に及ぼす影響には留意が必要である」との記述があった。2024～2025年は日銀が利上げを
続けていたのをよそに、欧米中銀が利下げ方向に動いていたため、世界全体でみれば金融環
境は緩和方向にあった。それに対して2026年後半以降、主要中銀は同時に金融引き締め方向
に動いており、世界全体として金融市場にストレスがかかりやすい状況にある。事実、最近
のＡＩ・半導体株の調整は、金利上昇による直接・間接の影響がある。たとえば、金利上昇
による資金調達コストの増加がＡＩ投資そのものを減速させ得る経路もあれば、安全資産で
ある国債利回りの上昇が株式の相対的な魅力を低下させ株価を下押しするという経路もあ
る。

・③については夏場頃から意識されていたことではあるが、予想以上に民主党が票を伸ばした
選挙結果を目の当たりにして、「ＡＩ規制」が強く意識された格好であろう。米国の経済成
長率をけん引するＡＩ投資が規制によって減速すれば、景気は減速する公算が大きい。事
実、ＩＴ関連投資（民間設備投資における情報技術装置・ソフトウェア）はＧＤＰ比で５％
を超えるに至っており、これはＩＴバブル期（90年代後半～2000年頃）よりも大きい。この
間、データセンターの建設支出も飛躍的に伸びている。株式市場では「ＡＩ規制」が政治的
な人質となり、成長のエンジンが止まってしまうことに対する懸念が強く意識されていると
みられる。もっとも、景気減速は、規制を進める側にとっても望ましい結果ではない。また
中国との開発競争で後塵を拝する恐れもある。ＡＩによる暴走が危惧される一方、強いＡＩ
を持たないことはサイバーセキュリティ上の問題に直結しかねないため、ＡＩ規制が一気に
強化される可能性は低いと判断される。

### Page 3

3/4

・最後に筆者が定点観測しているマクロデータから、ここ数日の下落を踏まえた半導体株の現
在位置を確認する（５日終値まで反映）。まず、台湾の輸出受注とＳＯＸ指数を比較する
と、その乖離が著しく拡大している。ＩＴ関連財を中心に受注額が伸び続ける一方、ＳＯＸ
指数は半導体不況を織り込むかのような動きとなっており、トランプ関税に揺れた2025年春
夏に匹敵するほどの乖離となっている。

・次に韓国の輸出金額とKOSPIを比較すると、こちらも株価はかなり悲観的な半導体市況の到来
を織り込んでいるようにみえる。信用取引やレバレッジ取引に絡んだ個人投資家の投げ売り
など、需給の悪さだけでは説明できない状況にある。

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
・ここで年央までのＡＩ・半導体相場で主役級の存在感を示してきたメモリ大手５社の株価動
向を確認する。５社の単純平均（均等ウェイト）株価は６月下旬の既往高値から半分程度の
水準まで滑落し、予想ＰＥＲは６月20日の9.6倍から直近では３倍台前半まで大きく低下して
おり、この尺度でみた割安感は強まっている。換言すれば、数年先の大幅減益を織り込み、
期待値が上がっていないことを意味する。12ヶ月先予想ＥＰＳは現在も拡大しており、近い
将来の大幅減益予想はコンセンサスではないが、２～３年以上先の業績悪化が相当織り込ま
れているようにみえる。

・相場反転のきっかけとしては、イラン情勢の好転に伴う原油価格の下落、およびＦｅｄを中
心とする主要中央銀行の利上げ中断・停止、米民主党幹部からのＡＩ規制に関する穏健な声
明などが考えられる。
